Let's look at previous SOTA LP approach: Fix f, solve LP for g maximizing sum g with f*g∞≤M. This is similar to multiplicative updates? We can implement a more aggressive version: alternating updates between f and g to reduce M. It might escape uniform? Starting from random/ heuristic. But need get below 1.5. Let's understand LP step. Given current f normalized S=√(2n)? They set M=||f*f||∞. Compute g≥0 maximizing ∑g subject to f*g_k ≤ M. This is linear: variables g, constraints ∑_j f_{k-j} g_j ≤ M. Then rescale g to normalization, convex combine with f. This step increases sum(g) beyond old while keeping product with f bounded. It chooses g as "dual" variables to spread mass where f small. It may reduce norm after convex update. Could implement with scipy.optimize.linprog for moderate n. But for n=6000, LP constraints 12000, variables6000, dense-ish banded; too slow. But we can use smaller coarse n and then upsample. However convex combination of same length; if we do LP on length 6121 perhaps infeasible. Need design.

This is a packing LP. The dual maybe choose weights? For large n, can use iterative combinatorial (multiplicative weights). But maybe not necessary.

Consider distribution as solution of integral equation F(t)=M for t in some interval, f>0. If F(x)=M on [a,b], then for any s,t in [a,b], ∫ f(u) f(s-u)=∫ f(u)f(t-u). This means the function f * (indicator?) Stationarity. If F constant on interval length L, then f*r? The variation of F is (f*f)' = f*(f') (where derivative in distribution sense). If (f*f)' =0 on interval, then convolution of f with f' vanishes there. This suggests f' shifted is supported outside interval. Hard.

Alternative use Fourier and uncertainty: f nonnegative L1. Can we make F small using oscillatory conjugate? f nonnegative cannot oscillate, but can have fine structure. If f is a sum of many small bumps, its convolution is sum of bump convolutions. If bumps are separated by nonlinearly designed distances, max peak may be sum of contributions. For f=N bumps of width δ, total probability 1. If bumps random far apart, sum distribution of two iid = self-convolution of point masses smoothed. The point part counts pairs of bumps. If choose bump positions as Sidon set, number of pair sums small, but total mass? Let's explore. Let f be N Dirac masses of weight 1/N at positions s_j in [0,1]. Convolution = (1/N^2)∑ p_t (Dirac). For random positions, expected pairs per t? For N points in continuous [0,1] with sum value t, pairs count ≈N^2 * density? Local search method A: Block multiplicative perturbations. Previous was too weak. We can enhance:
- Use "active ridge" updates: Identify peak conv index k_max. Move mass along the anti-diagonal that contributes to peak? For peak k, C_k = ∑ a_i a_{k-i}. To reduce C_k, decrease product pairs. In normalized setting, we can transfer mass from one element of a pair to another that does not increase other peaks. A simple move: choose i with high a_i paired with high a_j, shift small mass from i to an index r such that a_i decreases and a_r increases; net C_k change = δ(a_{r?} - a_j) plus second order. If choose r where a_{k-r} (paired value) is lower than a_j, we reduce. This is like sorting.
Could implement coordinate descent with line search:
For each peak k, for each i in valid pair, consider moving small mass from i to an alternate index r that currently has low "badness". But O(n^2).
Maybe use "clipping": Reduce high a_i where a_i * a_{k-i} contributes, and add to locations where gradient low. Projected subgradient does this.

Convolution via FFT:
```
def linear_convolution(a, b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    n=a.size; m=b.size
    L=1; while L < n+m-1: L <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb, L)[:n+m-1]
    # clean small neg
    c[np.abs(c)<1e-12]=0
    return c
```
Using pow2 L. For L=16384 okay. For many calls, allocation overhead. Could precompute rfft of a each iteration? We update a, not. For gradient using weights, need convolution of weights with a? We can compute C full and active top; gradient for active top via slicing O(Tn), no second FFT. Good.

Score:
```
def score_fft(a):
    a=np.asarray(a); s=a.sum(); if s<=0 return inf
    c=linear_convolution(a)
    m=max(0,c.max())
    return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```
Since a normalized sum1, score=2n maxc. If not normalized.

C. Upsample:
```
def upsample(p, n):
    if p.size==n: return p.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,p.size,endpoint=False)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
    # density f = p * p.size (since p ≈ f_i / d)
    f = p * p.size
    f_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, f, left=f[0], right=f[-1])
    # Maybe smooth with boxcar? 
    a=f_new/n
    return normalize(a)
```
Interpolation could create negative? f nonneg. Good.
Maybe use cubic? linear ok.

Given a normalized sum1 length n. Compute C=linear_convolution(a). M=C.max(). Active weights: choose top T by C (or soft). We need gradient g_j for M_soft = ∑ w_k C_k, with weights w sum1.
g_j = 2 ∑_k w_k a_{k-j} for valid k. We can compute for top T via slices:
```
def active_gradient(a, weights_indices):
    n=len(a)
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    for k,w in zip(indices, weights):
        # valid i=j, a[i] pairs with a[k-i]
        # For j from max(0, k-(n-1)) to min(n-1, k):
        lo = max(0, k-(n-1))
        hi = min(n-1, k)
        # a_{k-j} for j in [lo,hi] is a[k-lo : k-hi+1][::-1]
        seg = a[k-lo : k-hi+1][::-1]
        grad[lo:hi+1] += 2*w*seg
    return grad
```
For k central n-1: lo=0,hi=n-1, seg=a[n-1::-1], grad=2w reverse(a). Good.
Weights: We can use exponential based on C relative to M. Let q = C / C.max()? If M small. Use τ = M*0.05 maybe. w = exp((C - M)/τ) for all k? Sum huge? To avoid overflows, only top. Simpler top T with weights=1/T. But to smooth, use maybe top 32 weighted by C rank.

Multiplicative update:
```
def gradient_step(a, step, topT, c_tau=None):
   C=conv(a); M=C.max()
   idx=np.argsort(C)[-topT:][::-1]
   # weights: maybe w = exp((C[idx]-M)/(M*0.1+eps))/sum
   grad=active_gradient(a, idx, weights)
   # center gradient by mean (or median) to respect scale
   g=grad - grad.mean()
   # clip g to [-Gmax, Gmax]
   g=np.clip(g, -5,5)? Since grad ~1/n, range ~? Scale: g might range 1e-4. Center within ± maybe 2e-4. If clip 1, no effect. Need scale step.
   # Use relative: g_norm = (g - mean)/scale, scale=max(abs(g).max(),eps)
   ratio = np.exp(-eta * g_norm)
   a_new = a * ratio
   a_new[a_new<0]=0; a_new/=a_new.sum()
   return a_new, C, M
```

Profiles functions:
```
def make_profile(n, kind, rng=None):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
   if kind==0: a=np.ones(n)
   elif kind==1: # left high step p=0.5
       a=np.where(x<0.5, 4/3, 2/3)
   elif kind==2: # exponential left decay
       lam=1.0; a=np.exp(-lam*x); normalize?
   elif kind==3: # linear decreasing
       beta=1.0; a=1+beta*(0.5-x)
   elif kind==4: # hump left: exp -((x-c)^2)/w
   elif kind==5: # 3-level: high, med, low
       p1,p2=0.3,0.7; values optimized? We'll sweep.
   else random nonnegative.
   a=a+1e-6; return normalize
```

DE objective:
```
def eval_z(z):
   a=np.exp(z - z.max())
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=0: return inf
   a/=s
   # direct convolve for n<=512
   c=np.convolve(a,a)
   m=float(np.max(c))
   if m<0: m=0
   return 2*n*m  # since sum=1
```

Gradient multiplicative function:
```
def mm_mul(a, active_indices, weights=None, eta=0.1):
   n=a.size
   C=linear_convolution(a)  # could pass C to avoid recompute
   if weights is None: len indices
   g=np.zeros(n)
   for k,w in zip(indices, weights): ...
   g_mean = g.mean()
   g = g - g_mean
   sc = np.max(np.abs(g))
   if sc <= 1e-18: return a
   gn = g/sc
   ratio = np.exp(-eta * gn)
   a2 = a * ratio
   # clip negative, zeros? if all zero return a
   a2[a2<0]=0
   s=a2.sum(); if s<=0: return a
   return a2/s
```
But recomputing C inside; we can provide C from previous for current. For line search, after update need new C via score_fft.
Weights: We can choose top indices from C (descending). For eta, to keep centered, sc scaling. The actual magnitude of g_c/sc in [-1,1]; eta independent of n. Good.

Potential issue: Multiplicative update with centered gradient and then normalization can drastically change sum but normalized. It may create tiny values; okay. To avoid too many zeros, add floor? Not necessary; evaluator clips 0.

Choose eta schedule: start small 0.2, decrease if score worsens? We'll try list. To avoid many FFTs, for each iteration:
- Get C and M for current a. If M improved, update best.
- Sort top indices maybe top 32.
- Build weights: use `w = np.exp((C[idx] - M) / (M*0.05 + 1e-15)) / sum`. This emphasizes equal peaks.
- For eta in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5]: compute a2, score; choose best. If best < current, accept. Else break and try random perturbations.
But topT fixed 16/32. If no improvement, reduce T to 1 (focus strong) or increase to 128 (smooth). Use cycles.

Block perturbations:
```
def random_block_perturb(a, rng, score, deadline):
   n=a.size
   width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//2000), max(2,n//20)))
   start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width+1)))
   amp=rng.exponential(0.1)+0.05
   a2=a.copy()

def build_lp_matrix(h):
    n=h.size
    idx = np.arange(n)
    cols = np.tile(idx, n)
    rows = np.repeat(idx, n) + np.tile(idx, n)
    data = np.tile(h, n)
    return sp.coo_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*n-1, n)).tocsc()
```

   Implementation outline:
```
def active_local_search(a0, score0, deadline, rng):
    a = a0.copy(); score=score0; best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score
    n=a.size
    # precompute eta list
    etas = [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5]
    ts = [1,2,3,5,8,13,21,34,55]
    stuck=0; last_improve_score=score; last_improve_time=time.monotonic()
    temp=0.05
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if time.monotonic() - last_improve_time > 20:
           # random restart perturb
           ...
       C=convolve_real(a)
       m=float(np.max(C)) if C.size else inf
       s=2*n*m/(a.sum()**2) # sum normalization maybe a sum1
       if not finite: break
       if s < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=s; best_a=a.copy(); last_improve...; score=s
       # choose active T based on iteration/random
       # use top 64 for sorting
       if C.size > 64:
          top64 = np.argpartition(C, -64)[-64:]
          order = top64[np.argsort(C[top64])[::-1]]
       else:
          order = np.argsort(C)[::-1]
       T = ts[iteration % len(ts)]
       if T > len(order): T=len(order)
       idx = order[:T]
       # weights: soft over idx
       wvals = C[idx]
       sc = max(1e-12, (wvals.max()-wvals.min())*0.2)
       ws = np.exp((wvals-wvals.max())/sc)
       ws /= ws.sum()
       g = np.zeros(n)
       for k,w in zip(idx, ws):
          lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
          seg = a[k-lo:k-hi+1][::-1]
          g[lo:hi+1] += 2.0*w*seg
       # center and scale
       gmean = g.mean()
       g = g - gmean
       scg = np.max(np.abs(g)); if scg>1e-15: g/=scg
       improved=False
       for eta in etas:
           ratio = np.exp(-eta * g)
           a2 = a * ratio
           a2 = np.maximum(a2, 0.0)
           ssum = a2.sum()
           if ssum <=0: continue
           a2 /= ssum
           # If due to rounding score maybe
           s2 = quick_score(a2)
           if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < score - 1e-12:
               a=a2; score=s2; improved=True; break
       if improved: continue
       # Try random block perturb
       a2 = block_perturb(a, rng)
       s2=quick_score(a2)
       if s2 < score -1e-12:
           a=a2; score=s2; continue
       # Occasionally accept worse for annealing? For final maybe no.
       if rng.random() < 0.05 and temp>1e-4:
           if s2 < score + temp:
              a=a2; score=s2
       temp *=0.98
   return best_a, best_score
```
But note: We compute C before, then for each eta compute quick_score (convolve) which recomputes. That's okay.
Potential inefficiency: For each epoch, if no eta improves, we try block (convolve) and maybe worse (already have s2). Good.
Random restarts: if no improvement for 30s, perturb a with larger block/noise, reset.
```
def random_restart(a, rng, strength):
   n=a.size; a2=a.copy(); width=... large; a2[start:stop]*=exp(rng.normal(0,strength,width)); if rng.random()<0.3 mirror; normalize; return a2
```
Maybe after stuck, jump to a random profile upsampled? We can do a few in high-level stages.

Use of quick_score: It normalizes a2? quick_score uses sum squared. Ensure a2 normalized. a2 sum maybe 1; but for safety use sum.

Number of eps/large: Since n small or large, top64 sorting with C length ~2n. For n=6000 okay.

We must be careful with `active_local_search` for uniform: top active multiple; g centered may be zero if scalar? If g zero, ratio1 no improvement; then block perturb helps. If starting from nonuniform, okay.

Block perturbation function top-level:
```
def block_perturb(a, rng, min_width_frac=1/2000, max_width_frac=1/20, sigma=0.2):
    n=a.size; width=int(rng.integers(max(1,int(n*min_width_frac)), max(2,int(n*max_width_frac)+1)))
    start=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n-width+1)))
    segment = a[start:start+width]
    amp = float(rng.exponential(0.15)+0.05)
    mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, amp, size=segment.size))
    # avoid zeros: mult = np.where(mult<1e-6,1e-6,mult)
    a2=a.copy(); a2[start:start+width]=segment*mult
    # optionally mix with reversed sequence to decorrelate? 
    if rng.random()<0.15:
       a2 = 0.5*(a2 + a2[::-1])
    return normalize_positive(a2)
```
Maybe mirror mix enforces symmetry, not desired? Could be either. Use probability 0.1 only.
Also occasionally "global scale fft"? not.

High-level `propose_candidate`:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=980, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start+max(0,budget_s-10)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    #n_final selection
    try:
       global_seq=np.clip(np.asarray(height_sequence_1,dtype=float),0,1000)
    except: global_seq=np.ones(6121)
    n_global=global_seq.size if >0 else 6121
    n_final = int(n_global) if 1000<=n_global<=10000 else 6121
    # best exact/best fft
    best_a=np.ones(n_final); best_score=inf
    # evaluate global upsampled to n_final
    for arr in [global_seq[:n_final] if size>=n_final, upsample(global, n_final)]:...
       s=quick_score(arr); best...
       exact=evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist()); if exact<best_exact...
    # quick profiles final
    profiles=make_profiles(n_final, rng)
    for p in profiles: s=quick_score; update best; exact occasionally
    # small stage optimize
    small_n=256 (or 512 if time)
    small_profiles=make_profiles(small_n, rng)

```
def lp_improve(p0, n, max_it, deadline, verbose=False):
    p = normalize_positive(p0)
    score = quick_score(p)
    for it in range(max_it):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       h = n * p
       c = convolve_real(h)
       Mh = float(np.max(c))
       if not finite: break
       try build A
       res=linprog(...)
       if res.status !=0 or res.x is None: break
       z=np.maximum(res.x,0)
       sz=z.sum(); if sz<=0: break
       g=z/sz
       best_p=p; best_s=score
       for t in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.8]:
          pn=(1-t)*p + t*g; pn=normalize_positive(pn)
          sn=quick_score(pn)
          if finite and sn<best_s-1e-12:
             best_p=pn; best_s=sn
       if not np.any(best_p != p) and best_s>=score-1e-12:
           # try tighter RHS
           for ratio in [0.98,0.95]:
               try solve with Mh*ratio and update
           if no: break
       p=best_p; score=best_s
    return p, score
```

def differential_evolution_profile(n, seed, budget_s, known_profiles=None):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
    pop=[]; scores=[]
    # initial profiles as log
    if known_profiles: for a in top: z=np.log(a+1e-12)
    while len(pop)<min(64, 4*n): pop.append(rng.normal(size=n)*0.5)
    best_z=...
    F=0.7; CR=0.9; Cblend=0.2
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       for i in range(len(pop)):
           r1,r2,r3 = random distinct
           x = pop[r1] + F*(pop[r2]-pop[r3])
           # crossover per element
           mask = rng.random(n) < CR
           x = np.where(mask, x, pop[i])
           # attraction to best
           x = (1-Cblend)*x + Cblend*pop[best_idx]
           x = np.clip(x, -6, 6)
           s=quick_score_from_z(x)
           if s < scores[i]: replace
           if s < best_score: best...
       it+=1
    return best_a

def pair_equalize(a, k, alpha):
    n=a.size
    lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
    if lo>=hi: return a.copy()
    idx = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
    rev = k - idx
    # only update i<j to avoid double
    mask = idx < rev
    i = idx[mask]; j=rev[mask]
    ai=a[i]; aj=a[j]
    mean=0.5*(ai+aj)
    new_ai = (1-alpha)*ai + alpha*mean ??? Equalizing both to mean: new=ai + (mean-ai)*alpha = (1-α/2)ai? Wait mean=(ai+aj)/2. ai + α(mean-ai)= (1-α)ai + α*0.5(ai+aj)= (1-α/2)ai + (α/2)aj. Function `apply_equalize(a, peaks, alpha)` should apply peaks sequentially on current b. If an index pair updated multiple times, may change. Use small alpha. For central k, mapping involution; vectorized. For off-center, pairs i<j; if some index paired with itself (i=j) skip.
```
def apply_equalize(a, peaks, alpha):
    b=a.copy(); n=a.size; alpha=min(0.5,alpha)
    for k in peaks:
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       if lo>=hi: continue
       idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
       rev=k-idx
       mask=idx<rev
       if not mask.any(): continue
       i=idx[mask]; j=rev[mask]
       ai=b[i]; aj=b[j]
       b[i]=(1-alpha)*ai + alpha*aj
       b[j]=(1-alpha)*aj + alpha*ai
    return b
```
Since sum of each pair preserved, total sum preserved. If alpha=0.5 equalizes.
This operation can be seen as smoothing along anti-diagonals. It might be very effective: for each active peak, equalizing values reduces that peak quadratically. It also affects other peaks but maybe some improved. It is deterministic and may drive to a global-ish optimum.

Normalize: `normalize_positive` will clip and sum. If all zero, returns uniform.

Function `normalize_positive(a)`:
```
def normalize_positive(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    a[~np.isfinite(a)]=0.0
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-12:
       return np.full(a.size if a.size>0 else 1, 1.0/max(1,a.size))
    return a/s
```
If a empty? return ones(1)? But n known. Use if a.size==0: return np.ones(1). No sequences empty.

`quick_score(a)`:
```
def quick_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=1e-12 or not finite: return inf
    c=convolve_real(a)
    m=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if m<0: m=0.0
    return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```
Use sum not assume 1. Since candidates normalized, okay.

`convolve_real` for direct threshold 350. For FFT, if a size 1? L=1? 2n-1=1, (2).bit_length? Let's handle:
```
m=2*n-1
L=1
while L<m: L <<=1
```
Simple. For n up to 10000, bit loop negligible. Or use bit_length.
```
L = 1 << max(1, (2*n-2).bit_length())
```
If 2n-1=1 => n=1, 2n-2=0 bit_length0, max(1,0)=1 =>L=2, okay bigger.

`top_indices(c, t)`:
```
def top_indices(c, t):
    c=np.asarray(c)
    if c.size <= t:
       return np.argsort(c)[::-1].astype(int)
    t=min(t,c.size-1)
    part=np.argpartition(c, -t)[-t:]
    return part[np.argsort(c[part])[::-1]]
```
If c has NaN? no.

`active_gradient_from_peaks(a, peaks, weights)` top-level:
```
def active_gradient_from_peaks(a, peaks, weights):
    n=a.size; g=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float64)
    for idx,k in enumerate(peaks):
       k=int(k); w=float(weights[idx])
       lo = k - (n-1)
       if lo <0: lo=0
       hi = k
       if hi >= n: hi=n-1
       if lo>hi: continue
       seg = a[k-lo : k-hi+1][::-1]
       g[lo:hi+1] += (2.0*w)*seg
    return g
```
Check slice: For k=3,n=5, lo=0,hi=3, k-lo=3, k-hi=0, slice a[3:1]? Python a[3:1] empty. We need `a[k-lo : k-hi+1]` with start > stop gives empty if not reversed? For k=3, lo=0 -> start 3, hi=3 -> k-hi+1=1, start3 stop1 empty. That's wrong. We want for j=0..3: a[k-j]=[a3,a2,a1,a0]. This is `a[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1]`? Let's derive: indices for j=lo..hi, k-j from k-lo down to k-hi. So slice `a[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1]` if stop exclusive. For lo=0,hi=3 -> start3 stop -1? k-hi-1 = -1, slice a[3:-1:-1] empty due stop -1. Use `a[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1]` works if stop=-1 means before index0? For start3, stop -1, step -1 yields [3,2,1,0] because stop -1 exclusive? In Python `a[3:-1:-1]` returns [3,2,1]? If hi=n-1,k=n-1 -> stop=-1; `a[n-1:-1:-1]` gives [n-1,...,1]? Excludes 0. Need if k-hi-1 <0 use None. So:
```
start = k-lo

if stop < 0:
    seg = a[start::-1]
else:
    seg = a[start:stop:-1]
```

`active_local_search` implementation details:
Parameters: `a0, score0, deadline, rng, anneal=False`.
Use local `best_a`, `best_score`. At top, if score0 not finite, quick_score. Ensure a normalized. We can compute current score maybe score0.
Use `improve_threshold = 1e-10`.
Use iteration counter.
Need `time.monotonic()` many.
Pseudo in code:
```
def active_local_search(a0, score0, deadline, rng, anneal=False):
    a = np.asarray(a0,dtype=np.float64).copy(); a=normalize_positive(a)
    score = quick_score(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score
    n=a.size
    temp=1e-3 if anneal else 0.0
   _stuck_time = time.monotonic()
    iteration=0
    etas=(0.05,0.12,0.25,0.5,1.0)
    t_cycle=(1,2,4,8,16)
    equal_specs=((1, 0.15,0.4), (2,0.08,0.2), (4,0.03,0.08)) # maybe
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       iteration +=1
       C=convolve_real(a)
       if C.size==0: break
       m=float(np.max(C)); if m<0: m=0
       s=current? score=2*n*m/(a.sum()**2) # but a normalized ~1; use a.sum for safety.
       if not finite: break
       if s < best_score - 1e-10:
          best_a=a.copy(); best_score=s; _stuck_time=now
       order=top_indices(C, 16)
       improved=False
       # equalize candidates
       for spec in equal_specs:
           count=spec[0]; a1=spec[1]; a2=spec[2]
           if count > order.size: count=order.size
           peaks=order[:count]
           if peaks.size==0: break
           for alpha in (a1,a2):
              cand=apply_equalize(a, peaks, alpha)
              sc=quick_score(cand)
              if finite and sc < score - 1e-10:
                 a=cand; score=sc; improved=True; break
           if improved: break
       if improved: continue
       # gradient
       T=t_cycle[iteration % len(t_cycle)]
       T=max(1,min(T, order.size))
       peaks=order[:T]
       vals=C[peaks]
       rngvals = float(vals.max()-vals.min()) if vals.size else 0
       tau = max(1e-12, 0.15*rngvals + 1e-12)
       w=np.exp((vals-vals.max())/tau); w/=w.sum()
       g=active_gradient_from_peaks(a, peaks, w)
       gmean=float(g.mean())
       g = g - gmean
       scg=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
       if scg > 1e-15:
          g = g/scg
          for eta in etas:
             ratio=np.exp(-eta*g)
             cand=a*ratio
             cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0)
             ss=cand.sum()
             if ss>1e-12:
                cand=cand/ss
                sc=quick_score(cand)

                   a=cand; score=sc; improved=True; break
       if improved: continue
       # block perturb

       sc=quick_score(cand)
       if finite and sc < score - 1e-10:

       elif anneal and temp>1e-5 and sc finite and sc < score + temp and rng.random()<0.3:
           a=cand; score=sc

           temp *=0.99 if anneal else 1.0
           if time.monotonic() - _stuck_time > 25:

                       a=cand; score=sc; break

    if quick? At the end return best_a, best_score. Maybe current a better than best? best updated when C computed. If last no C? okay.

Implement:
```
def resample_sequence(a, n):
    a=normalize_positive(a)
    m=a.size
    if m == n: return a
    if m == 1: return np.full(n, 1.0/n)
    if m < n:
       x0=np.linspace(0,1,m,endpoint=False)
       x1=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
       f=a*m
       f1=np.interp(x1, x0, f)
       out=f1/n
    else:
       # downsample by averaging blocks roughly
       blocks = np.linspace(0,m,n+1).astype(int)
       out=np.empty(n)
       for i in range(n):
          out[i]=a[blocks[i]:blocks[i+1]].mean() if blocks[i+1]>blocks[i] else a[blocks[i]]
    return normalize_positive(out)
```
No nested.

Maybe applying a slight smoothing to upsampled profile helps: `out = np.convolve(out, np.array([0.25,0.5,0.25]), mode='same')` but convolution with same length assumes boundary; normalize. It may reduce peaks. But can also blur. We can optionally smooth if input coarse m<max(64,n) and n>1000: apply Gaussian? Keep simple no.

`convolve_real`:
```
def convolve_real(a, b=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if b is None: b=a
    else: b=np.asarray(b,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size; m=b.size
    if n==0 or m==0: return np.empty(0)
    if n<=350 and (b is a or m<=350):
       c=np.convolve(a,b)
       c[~np.isfinite(c)]=0.0
       return c
    L=1
    target=n+m-1
    while L<target: L <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    if b is a: fb=fa
    else: fb=np.fft.rfft(b,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,L)
    c=c[:target]
    c[~np.isfinite(c)]=0.0
    return c
```
For b is a, `fa*fb` uses complex. OK.

idx = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
rev = k - idx
mask = idx < rev
i = idx[mask]; j = rev[mask]

ai=b[i]; aj=b[j]
b[i] = (1-alpha)*ai + alpha*aj
b[j] = (1-alpha)*aj + alpha*ai
```

    deadline=time.monotonic()+max(0,budget_s)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    pop=[]; scores=[]

       z=np.log(a+1e-12); pop.append(z); scores.append(quick_score(a))
    while len(pop)<24: z=rng.normal(0,0.5,n); pop.append(z); scores.append(score_from_z(z))
    best_idx=0; best_score=min(scores)
    pop[best_idx] maybe.
    it=0; last_best=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       for i in range(len(pop)):
          r1 = rng.integers(0, len(pop))
          r2 = rng.integers(0, len(pop))
          r3 = rng.integers(0, len(pop))
          if r1==r2: r2=(r2+1)%len(pop)
          if r1==r3 or r2==r3: r3=(r3+1)%len(pop)
          if r1==r3: r3=(r3+1)%len(pop) # still could, but okay

          mut = 0.8*mut +0.2*pop[best_idx]

          sc=score_from_z(mut)
          if sc < scores[i]:

                best_score=sc; best_idx=i; last_best=time.monotonic()

```
def add_candidate(candidates, a, target_n, max_score):
    if a is None: return
    arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size==0: return
    if target_n is not None and arr.size != target_n:
       arr = resample_sequence(arr, target_n)
    else:
       arr = normalize_positive(arr)
    s=quick_score(arr)
    if np.isfinite(s) and s <= max_score + 0.25:
       candidates.append((s, len(candidates), arr))
```

Need top-level function `update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, arr)`:
```
def update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, arr):
    if arr is None: return exact_score, exact_a
    s=exact_eval(arr)
    if np.isfinite(s) and s < exact_score:
       return s, np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    return exact_score, exact_a
```

`exact_eval` uses evaluate_sequence. If arr is numpy, `.tolist()`. Ensure list of floats not bool. `np.asarray(arr,dtype=float)` then `.tolist()`.
```
def exact_eval(arr):
    try:
       a=np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
       a[a<0]=0
       if a.size==0: return inf
       return float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
    except Exception:
       return float('inf')
```

def build_lp_matrix(h):
   n=h.size
   idx=np.arange(n)
   rows=np.repeat(idx,n)+np.tile(idx,n)
   cols=np.tile(idx,n)
   data=np.tile(h,n)
   return sp.coo_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n)).tocsc()

```python
"""
Step function coefficient search for minimizing

    evaluate_sequence(a) = 2 * n * max(a * a) / (sum(a))^2.

The search combines:
  - deterministic analytic/profile initializers,
  - small-scale differential evolution,
  - active-lag pair equalization moves,
  - sub-gradient multiplicative moves,
  - random block perturbations,
  - a coarse linear-programming alternating relaxation inspired by the
    previous SOTA,
  - resampling to a longer final length and exact final evaluation.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    import scipy.optimize as osp
    import scipy.sparse as sp
except Exception:
    osp = None
    sp = None


def normalize_positive(a):
    """Return a nonnegative normalized version of a."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-14:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def convolve_real(a, b=None):
    """Real linear convolution, using direct for small n and FFT otherwise."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if b is None:
        b = a
    else:
        b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    m = b.size
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    # Direct convolution is cheap for these sizes and avoids FFT artifacts.
    if n <= 512 and m <= 512:
        c = np.convolve(a, b)
        c[~np.isfinite(c)] = 0.0
        return c

    target = n + m - 1
    L = 1
    while L < target:
        L <<= 1

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    if b is a:
        fb = fa
    else:
        fb = np.fft.rfft(b, L)

    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, L)
    c = c[:target]
    c[~np.isfinite(c)] = 0.0
    return c


def quick_score(a):
    """Fast FFT/direct score, using the same formula as evaluate_sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-14:
        return float("inf")

    c = convolve_real(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))


def exact_eval(a):
    """Call the authoritative evaluator safely."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            return float("inf")
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
        if float(arr.sum()) < 0.01:
            return float("inf")
        return float(evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, a):
    """Evaluate a exactly and keep the exact best pair."""
    if a is None:
        return exact_score, exact_a
    s = exact_eval(a)
    if np.isfinite(s) and s < exact_score:
        return s, np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    return exact_score, exact_a


def top_indices(c, t):
    """Indices of the largest t entries, largest first."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int)
    t = min(int(t), int(c.size))
    if t <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int)
    if c.size <= t:
        return np.argsort(c)[::-1].astype(int)
    part = np.argpartition(c, -t)[-t:]
    part = part[np.argsort(c[part])[::-1]]
    return part.astype(int)


def apply_equalize(a, peaks, alpha):
    """
    For each active convolution lag k, move each valid pair (i, k-i)
    toward each other. For fixed pair sum, this reduces that lag.
    """
    b = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = b.size
    if n == 0:
        return b
    alpha = float(alpha)
    if not np.isfinite(alpha):
        return b
    alpha = min(max(alpha, 0.0), 0.5)
    if alpha <= 1e-12:
        return b

    for kk in peaks:
        k = int(kk)
        if k < 0:
            continue
        lo = k - (n - 1)
        if lo < 0:
            lo = 0
        hi = k
        if hi >= n:
            hi = n - 1
        if lo >= hi:
            continue

        idx = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
        rev = k - idx
        mask = idx < rev
        if not np.any(mask):
            continue
        i = idx[mask]
        j = rev[mask]
        ai = b[i]
        aj = b[j]
        b[i] = (1.0 - alpha) * ai + alpha * aj
        b[j] = (1.0 - alpha) * aj + alpha * ai

    return normalize_positive(b)


def active_gradient_from_peaks(a, peaks, weights):
    """
    Weighted subgradient for max-weighted convolution lags.

    For C_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}, dC_k/da_j = 2 a_{k-j} where valid.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0:
        return g

    for idx, kk in enumerate(peaks):
        k = int(kk)
        w = float(weights[idx])
        if w <= 0.0:
            continue

        lo = k - (n - 1)
        if lo < 0:
            lo = 0
        hi = k
        if hi >= n:
            hi = n - 1
        if lo > hi:
            continue

        start = k - lo
        stop = k - hi - 1
        if stop < 0:
            seg = a[start::-1]
        else:
            seg = a[start:stop:-1]

        if seg.size == hi - lo + 1:
            g[lo:hi + 1] += (2.0 * w) * seg

    return g


def block_perturb(a, rng):
    """Random multiplicative perturbation of one contiguous block."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    lo_w = max(1, int(0.001 * n))
    hi_w = int(max(2, 0.08 * n))
    hi_w = min(hi_w, n)
    if lo_w > hi_w:
        lo_w = hi_w = 1

    width = int(rng.integers(lo_w, hi_w + 1))
    start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))

    amp = 0.08 + 0.35 * float(rng.random())
    mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, amp, width))
    mult[~np.isfinite(mult)] = 1.0
    mult = np.maximum(mult, 1e-10)

    b = a.copy()
    b[start:start + width] *= mult

    if rng.random() < 0.10:
        b = 0.5 * (b + b[::-1])

    return normalize_positive(b)


def tiny_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.012):
    """Very small global multiplicative noise, used to escape exact stagnation."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    b = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return normalize_positive(b)


def active_local_search(a0, score0, deadline, rng, anneal=False):
    """
    Greedy local search with active-lag equalization, subgradient multiplicative
    updates, and random block moves.  Tracks the best sequence even when the
    current state wanders.
    """
    a = normalize_positive(a0)
    if a.size == 0:
        a = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    score = quick_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        score = float("inf")

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score

    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return best_a, best_score

    temp = 1e-2 if anneal else 0.0
    no_improve = 0
    max_no_improve = 25 if anneal else 35

    etas = (0.08, 0.20, 0.45, 1.0)
    t_cycle = (1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13, 21)
    equal_specs = (
        (1, (0.20, 0.50)),
        (2, (0.08, 0.22)),
        (4, (0.03, 0.08)),
    )

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        C = convolve_real(a)
        if C.size == 0:
            break

        s = float(a.sum())
        if s <= 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(s):
            break
        m = float(np.max(C))
        if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
            m = 0.0
        current = float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))
        if not np.isfinite(current):
            break

        if current < best_score - 1e-10:
            best_score = current
            best_a = a.copy()

        order = top_indices(C, 20)
        if order.size == 0:
            break

        improved = False

        # 1) Active-lag pair equalization candidates.
        for count, alphas in equal_specs:
            if count > order.size:
                count = order.size
            peaks = order[:count]
            if peaks.size == 0:
                continue
            for alpha in alphas:
                cand = apply_equalize(a, peaks, alpha)
                sc = quick_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current - 1e-10:
                    a = cand
                    current = sc
                    score = sc
                    improved = True
                    break
            if improved:
                break

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        # 2) Weighted subgradient multiplicative step.
        T = int(t_cycle[no_improve % len(t_cycle)])
        T = max(1, min(T, int(order.size)))
        peaks = order[:T]
        vals = C[peaks]
        if vals.size:
            vrange = float(np.max(vals) - np.min(vals))
            tau = max(1e-12, 0.18 * vrange + 1e-12)
            w = np.exp((vals - np.max(vals)) / tau)
            wsum = float(w.sum())
            if wsum > 0.0 and np.isfinite(wsum):
                w = w / wsum
                g = active_gradient_from_peaks(a, peaks, w)
                gmean = float(g.mean())
                g = g - gmean
                gscale = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
                if gscale > 1e-15:
                    g = g / gscale
                    for eta in etas:
                        ratio = np.exp(-eta * g)
                        cand = a * ratio
                        cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
                        csum = float(cand.sum())
                        if csum > 1e-14 and np.isfinite(csum):
                            cand = cand / csum
                            sc = quick_score(cand)
                            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current - 1e-10:
                                a = cand
                                current = sc
                                score = sc
                                improved = True
                                break

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        # 3) Random block perturbation.
        cand = block_perturb(a, rng)
        sc = quick_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current - 1e-10:
            a = cand
            score = sc
            current = sc
            improved = True
        elif anneal and temp > 1e-6 and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < current + temp:
            if rng.random() < 0.65:
                a = cand
                score = sc
                current = sc

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if anneal:
                temp *= 0.992
            if no_improve >= max_no_improve:
                # Try a few exploratory moves, but keep the global best.
                accepted = False
                for _ in range(6):
                    cand2 = block_perturb(a, rng)
                    sc2 = quick_score(cand2)
                    limit = current + (0.05 if anneal else 0.008)
                    if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < limit:
                        a = cand2
                        score = sc2
                        current = sc2
                        accepted = True
                        no_improve = 0
                        break
                if not accepted:
                    cand3 = tiny_perturb(a, rng)
                    sc3 = quick_score(cand3)
                    if np.isfinite(sc3):
                        a = cand3
                        score = sc3
                        current = sc3
                        no_improve = 0
                    else:
                        break
            if anneal and no_improve >= max_no_improve * 2:
                break

    # Make sure the final current state is also considered.
    final_score = quick_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(final_score) and final_score < best_score:
        best_score = final_score
        best_a = a.copy()

    return best_a, best_score


def make_profiles(n, rng):
    """A diverse set of nonnegative normalized profiles."""
    profiles = []
    if n <= 0:
        return [np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)]

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)

    # Uniform baseline.
    profiles.append(normalize_positive(np.ones(n)))

    # Two-level left-heavy step profiles.
    for p in (0.35, 0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.65):
        for ratio in (1.2, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
            a = np.where(x < p, ratio, 1.0)
            profiles.append(normalize_positive(a))

    # Exponentially decaying profiles.
    for lam in (0.25, 0.55, 0.90, 1.4, 2.2):
        profiles.append(normalize_positive(np.exp(-lam * x)))
        profiles.append(normalize_positive(0.05 + 0.95 * np.exp(-lam * x)))

    # Linear profiles.
    for beta in (0.3, 0.7, 1.1, 1.5, 1.9):
        a = 1.0 + beta * (0.5 - x)
        profiles.append(normalize_positive(a))

    # Power-law decreasing profiles.
    for base in (0.0, 0.08, 0.18, 0.32):
        for alpha in (0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.8):
            a = base + (1.0 - base) * np.power(np.maximum(1.0 - x, 1e-8), alpha)
            profiles.append(normalize_positive(a))

    # Cosine modulations.
    for freq in (1, 2, 3):
        for amp in (0.15, 0.35, 0.60, 0.85):
            a = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * freq * x)
            profiles.append(normalize_positive(a))
    for amp in (0.25, 0.55, 0.85):
        a = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(np.pi * x)
        profiles.append(normalize_positive(a))

    # Local bumps.
    for c in (0.15, 0.30, 0.50, 0.70):
        for w in (0.07, 0.14, 0.28):
            a = 0.05 + np.exp(-((x - c) ** 2) / (2.0 * w * w))
            profiles.append(normalize_positive(a))

    # Random smooth-ish profiles.
    for _ in range(4):
        z = rng.normal(0.0, 0.8, n)
        z = np.convolve(z / max(1, n), np.ones(max(1, n // 64 + 1)), mode="same")
        a = np.exp(z)
        a = 0.1 + 0.9 * a
        profiles.append(normalize_positive(a))

    return [p for p in profiles if p is not None and p.size == n]


def resample_sequence(a, n):
    """Resample a probability-like sequence to length n."""
    a = normalize_positive(a)
    m = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return a.copy()
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)

    if m < n:
        x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, endpoint=False)
        x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
        f = a * float(m)
        f1 = np.interp(x1, x0, f)
        out = f1 / float(n)
    else:
        edges = np.linspace(0.0, float(m), n + 1).astype(int)
        out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for i in range(n):
            seg = a[edges[i]:edges[i + 1]]
            if seg.size == 0:
                out[i] = a[edges[i] % max(1, m)]
            else:
                out[i] = float(seg.mean())

    return normalize_positive(out)


def score_from_z(z):
    """Score a log-profile vector."""
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    if z.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    v = z - float(np.max(z))
    a = np.exp(v)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-14:
        return float("inf")
    a = a / s
    return quick_score(a)


def de_search(n, budget_s, seed, initial_profiles):
    """Small-dimensional differential evolution over log-profiles."""
    if budget_s <= 0.0 or n <= 0:
        return None, float("inf")

    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(budget_s)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    pop = []
    scores = []

    for prof in initial_profiles:
        arr = normalize_positive(prof)
        if arr.size != n:
            arr = resample_sequence(arr, n)
        if arr.size == n:
            z = np.log(arr + 1e-12)
            z[~np.isfinite(z)] = 0.0
            pop.append(z)
            scores.append(quick_score(arr))

    while len(pop) < 28:
        z = rng.normal(0.0, 0.6, n)
        z = np.clip(z, -6.0, 6.0)
        pop.append(z)
        scores.append(score_from_z(z))

    best_idx = 0
    best_score = min(scores)
    last_improve = time.monotonic()
    iteration = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        P = len(pop)
        for i in range(P):
            r1 = int(rng.integers(0, P))
            r2 = int(rng.integers(0, P))
            r3 = int(rng.integers(0, P))
            if r1 == r2:
                r2 = (r2 + 1) % P
            if r1 == r3 or r2 == r3:
                r3 = (r3 + 1) % P
            if r1 == r3:
                r3 = (r3 + 1) % P

            mut = pop[r1] + 0.80 * (pop[r2] - pop[r3])
            cross = rng.random(n) < 0.85
            mut = np.where(cross, mut, pop[i])
            mut = 0.82 * mut + 0.18 * pop[best_idx]
            mut = np.clip(mut, -6.0, 6.0)

            sc = score_from_z(mut)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < scores[i]:
                scores[i] = sc
                pop[i] = mut
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_idx = i
                    last_improve = time.monotonic()

        iteration += 1
        if iteration % 12 == 0 and time.monotonic() - last_improve > 8.0:
            half = max(1, len(pop) // 3)
            for i in range(half):
                z = rng.normal(0.0, 0.85, n)
                z = np.clip(z, -6.0, 6.0)
                pop[i] = z
                scores[i] = score_from_z(z)

    best_a = np.exp(pop[best_idx] - float(np.max(pop[best_idx])))
    best_a = normalize_positive(best_a)
    return best_a, best_score


def build_lp_matrix(h):
    """Sparse matrix A such that A @ z = h * z."""
    n = h.size
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    rows = np.repeat(idx, n) + np.tile(idx, n)
    cols = np.tile(idx, n)
    data = np.tile(h, n)
    return sp.coo_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n)).tocsc()


def lp_improve(p0, n, max_it, deadline):
    """
    Coarse alternating relaxation:
      maximize sum(z) s.t. h*z <= max(h*h), z >= 0,
      where h = n*p.
    Then mix p with z/sum(z).
    """
    if osp is None or sp is None:
        p = normalize_positive(p0)
        return p, quick_score(p)

    p = normalize_positive(p0)
    if p.size != n:
        p = resample_sequence(p, n)
    score = quick_score(p)

    for it in range(max_it):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        h = n * p
        ch = convolve_real(h)
        Mh = float(np.max(ch))
        if not np.isfinite(Mh) or Mh <= 0.0:
            break

        try:
            A = build_lp_matrix(h)
        except Exception:
            break

        rhs_candidates = (Mh, 0.97 * Mh)
        improved = False
        best_p = p
        best_s = score

        for rhs in rhs_candidates:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            try:
                bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
                res = osp.linprog(
                    -np.ones(n),
                    A_ub=A,
                    b_ub=np.full(2 * n - 1, float(rhs)),
                    bounds=bounds,
                    method="highs",
                )
            except Exception:
                res = None

            if res is None or res.status != 0 or res.x is None:
                continue

            z = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            z = np.clip(z, 0.0, None)
            z[~np.isfinite(z)] = 0.0
            sz = float(z.sum())
            if sz <= 1e-12:
                continue

            g = z / sz
            for t in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.55, 0.80):
                q = (1.0 - t) * p + t * g
                q = normalize_positive(q)
                s = quick_score(q)
                if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s - 1e-10:
                    best_s = s
                    best_p = q
                    improved = True

        if not improved:
            break

        p = best_p
        score = best_s

    return p, score


def add_candidate(candidates, a, target_n, max_score):
    """Add a candidate sequence, optionally resampled to target_n."""
    if a is None:
        return
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return

    if target_n is not None and arr.size != int(target_n):
        arr = resample_sequence(arr, int(target_n))
    else:
        arr = normalize_positive(arr)

    s = quick_score(arr)
    if np.isfinite(s) and s <= max_score + 0.25:
        candidates.append((s, len(candidates), arr))


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1600, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.  Returns a list of nonnegative heights.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = min(float(budget_s), 990.0)
    except Exception:
        budget = 990.0

    if budget <= 30.0:
        budget = 980.0
    deadline = start + max(20.0, budget - 10.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Get the previously known construction, if available.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    global_a = None
    try:
        gs = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        gs = np.clip(gs, 0.0, 1000.0)
        gs = gs[np.isfinite(gs)]
        if gs.size > 0:
            global_a = normalize_positive(gs)
    except Exception:
        global_a = None

    # Choose a final long length.  If the supplied construction has a
    # reasonable length, keep it.
    n_final = 6121
    if global_a is not None:
        gl = int(global_a.size)
        if 1000 <= gl <= 12000:
            n_final = gl
        elif gl > 0:
            n_final = int(min(9000, max(3000, gl)))

    best_a = np.ones(n_final, dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = float("inf")

    exact_score = float("inf")
    exact_a = None

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Quick exact/best from known construction.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if global_a is not None:
        if int(global_a.size) <= 15000:
            exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, global_a)

        g = resample_sequence(global_a, n_final)
        s = quick_score(g)
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
            best_score = s
            best_a = g.copy()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Parametric profiles at final length.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    final_profiles = make_profiles(n_final, rng)
    for prof in final_profiles:
        s = quick_score(prof)
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
            best_score = s
            best_a = prof.copy()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Small-scale optimization: DE + annealed active local search.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    small_budget = 0.0
    if remaining > 600.0:
        small_budget = 130.0
    elif remaining > 450.0:
        small_budget = 80.0
    elif remaining > 300.0:
        small_budget = 45.0

    small_a = None
    small_score = float("inf")

    if small_budget > 15.0:
        stage_deadline = time.monotonic() + small_budget
        small_n = 384

        small_profiles = make_profiles(small_n, rng)
        scored_small = []
        for idx, prof in enumerate(small_profiles):
            s = quick_score(prof)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                scored_small.append((s, idx, prof))
        scored_small.sort()

        if scored_small:
            small_a = scored_small[0][2].copy()
            small_score = scored_small[0][0]
            exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, small_a)

            search_list = []
            for item in scored_small[:4]:
                search_list.append(item[2])

            # Optional DE, only if there is real time.
            de_a = None
            if small_budget > 45.0:
                de_a, de_s = de_search(
                    small_n,
                    min(25.0, 0.25 * small_budget),
                    int(seed) + 1,
                    [small_a],
                )
                if de_a is not None and np.isfinite(de_s) and de_s < small_score:
                    small_a = de_a
                    small_score = de_s
                    exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, small_a)
                if de_a is not None:
                    search_list.append(de_a)

            # Annealed local search on several small starts.
            count = max(1, len(search_list))
            per = max(4.0, (stage_deadline - time.monotonic()) / float(count))
            for arr in search_list:
                if time.monotonic() >= stage_deadline - 3.0:
                    break
                s = quick_score(arr)
                sub_deadline = min(stage_deadline, time.monotonic() + per)
                a2, s2 = active_local_search(arr, s, sub_deadline, rng, anneal=True)
                if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < small_score:
                    small_score = s2
                    small_a = a2
                    exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, small_a)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Coarse LP relaxation, if scipy is available.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    lp_budget = 0.0
    if remaining > 500.0:
        lp_budget = 60.0
    elif remaining > 350.0:
        lp_budget = 30.0

    lp_a = None
    lp_score = float("inf")

    if lp_budget > 12.0 and osp is not None and sp is not None:
        stage_deadline = time.monotonic() + lp_budget
        n_lp = 512

        lp_inputs = []
        for arr in (small_a, best_a, global_a):
            if arr is not None:
                lp_inputs.append(resample_sequence(arr, n_lp))

        scored_lp = []
        for idx, arr in enumerate(lp_inputs):
            s = quick_score(arr)
            if np.isfinite(s) and s <= min(best_score, small_score + 0.25) + 0.10:
                scored_lp.append((s, idx, arr))
        scored_lp.sort()

        # If current best is still somewhat high, include a few current starts.
        if len(scored_lp) < 2 and best_a is not None:
            scored_lp.append((best_score, len(scored_lp), resample_sequence(best_a, n_lp)))

        count = max(1, min(2, len(scored_lp)))
        per = max(8.0, (stage_deadline - time.monotonic()) / float(count))
        for item in scored_lp[:max(2, count)]:
            if time.monotonic() >= stage_deadline - 3.0:
                break
            sub_deadline = min(stage_deadline, time.monotonic() + per)
            a2, s2 = lp_improve(item[2], n_lp, max_it=120, deadline=sub_deadline)
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < lp_score:
                lp_score = s2
                lp_a = a2
                exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, lp_a)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Assemble long and short candidates for final polishing.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    long_cands = []
    short_cands = []

    threshold = best_score + 0.25
    if not np.isfinite(threshold):
        threshold = float("inf")

    for arr in (global_a, best_a, small_a, lp_a):
        if arr is None:
            continue
        add_candidate(long_cands, arr, n_final, threshold)

    for arr in (small_a, lp_a):
        if arr is None:
            continue
        add_candidate(short_cands, arr, None, threshold)

    # Also add the current best exact candidate, if it is short or should be resampled.
    if exact_a is not None:
        add_candidate(long_cands, exact_a, n_final, threshold)
        add_candidate(short_cands, exact_a, None, threshold)

    long_cands.sort()
    short_cands.sort()

    # If long/short lists are somehow empty, use safe fallbacks.
    if not long_cands:
        add_candidate(long_cands, best_a, n_final, float("inf"))
    if not short_cands:
        add_candidate(short_cands, best_a, None, float("inf"))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final polishing: primarily one long candidate, then one short
    # candidate, then a long alternate.  Exact tracking is maintained.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    remain_total = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remain_total > 35.0:
        frac1 = 0.55
        frac2 = 0.30
        frac3 = 0.15

        if int(long_cands[0][2].size) == n_final:
            a2, s2 = active_local_search(
                long_cands[0][2],
                long_cands[0][0],
                min(deadline - 10.0, time.monotonic() + frac1 * remain_total),
                rng,
                anneal=False,
            )
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
                best_score = s2
                best_a = a2
            exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, a2)

        remain = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if int(short_cands[0][2].size) <= 1200 and remain > 20.0:
            a2, s2 = active_local_search(
                short_cands[0][2],
                short_cands[0][0],
                min(deadline - 10.0, time.monotonic() + frac2 * remain),
                rng,
                anneal=False,
            )
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
                best_score = s2
                best_a = a2
            exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, a2)

        remain = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if len(long_cands) > 1 and remain > 25.0:
            a2, s2 = active_local_search(
                long_cands[1][2],
                long_cands[1][0],
                min(deadline - 10.0, time.monotonic() + frac3 * remain),
                rng,
                anneal=False,
            )
            if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
                best_score = s2
                best_a = a2
            exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, a2)

        # One last restart on the current FFT-best if time remains.
        remain = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remain > 25.0:
            a_start = residual = None
            if best_a is not None and best_a.size == n_final:
                a_start = best_a
            elif long_cands:
                a_start = long_cands[0][2]
            if a_start is not None:
                a2, s2 = active_local_search(
                    a_start,
                    quick_score(a_start),
                    deadline - 4.0,
                    rng,
                    anneal=False,
                )
                if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
                    best_score = s2
                    best_a = a2
                exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, a2)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final exact evaluation of the few most promising candidates.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, best_a)
    if long_cands:
        exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, long_cands[0][2])
    if short_cands:
        exact_score, exact_a = update_exact_best(exact_score, exact_a, short_cands[0][2])

    if exact_a is not None:
        out = np.asarray(exact_a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    else:
        out = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()

    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 0.0
    s = float(out.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        out = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        out = out / s

    # Small safety: not too close to zero sum and reasonable length.
    if float(out.sum()) < 0.01:
        out = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
